Deliver fact-grounded, review-by-exception application preparation, measured assisted submission,
and authoritative owner-scoped technical history through confirmed submission for supported ATS
platforms.

## Definition of done (added 2026-09-19)

The Phase 3 exit sentence is the test: a candidate can prepare, skim, approve, submit, confirm, and review technical history on a supported ATS site. A task is **done** only when its part of that journey works through the deployed web app and the installed extension for a person, and the evidence names the journey step. Library tests, API-level synthetic acceptance, and screens that stop before the next step are recorded as **layer evidence** and never as done. Before asking the owner to try something, state plainly what it does and does not do yet.

## Deployment posture

Keep document and AI work on bounded queues/workers with concurrency limits inside the lean
runtime. Add dedicated AI/rendering capacity only from queue, latency, isolation, or model-provider
evidence; durable workflow, factuality, and approval controls are never scaled down.

9 unit tests. **Remaining:** Profile-owned vault persistence with a separate encryption key, vault API, and consent/deletion/export participation, blocked by D3-015 and D3-R4. **Vault persistence built and locally verified (2026-09-28), generated-synthetic data only:** [restricted answer vault](../../../backend/services/profile/src/restricted-answer-vault.ts) (Profile migration `0010`: an append-only, immutable-by-trigger versions table matching `profile_fact_versions`'s pattern, one active entry per owner/field/context via a partial unique index) and the [KMS cipher](../../../backend/gateway/src/restricted-answer-kms-cipher.ts) (AWS KMS `Encrypt`/`Decrypt` with `EncryptionContext` bound to the exact owner, field, and context, so a ciphertext can never be decrypted after being moved to a different row, even by a caller with database write access). **Owner-approved design (2026-09-28, "Approve, start building"):** a dedicated KMS key, separate from the shared database key; values encrypted directly (no per-record data key, since restricted answers are short scalars well under KMS's 4096-byte limit); no plaintext hash is ever stored, because several restricted answers have a tiny value space (yes/no, a short enum) and a hash would let a holder of the row brute-force the value without calling KMS. Concurrency is optimistic, matching Applications' `expectedRevision`: the caller supplies the version it last read (0 to create), and a mismatch is a conflict to retry, never a silent overwrite; encryption happens before any database transaction opens, so a slow KMS round trip never holds a connection or an advisory lock. Deleting carries the existing ciphertext forward into a new `deleted` version with no new KMS call. **Consent, export, and deletion participation:** the record's own existence is the consent required by ADR-010 ("only when the candidate supplies it and chooses to save it"); `profile.export_candidate_data` now returns each entry's metadata (field key, context, value type, sensitivity, state, version, timestamps) but deliberately omits the ciphertext, which is meaningless without the cipher - decrypting into the export is a tracked follow-up, not done here; `profile.delete_candidate_data` now deletes the owner's entries explicitly and returns the count. Tests: Profile unit 95 (13 new, including that no cipher call happens before input validation), Profile real-PostgreSQL integration 19 (9 new: create, correct in place, stale-version conflict, two contexts and two owners kept separate, delete-and-stops-matching, cross-owner and unknown-id refusal, the immutable-versions trigger, and export/deletion participation with the ciphertext confirmed absent from the export), Gateway unit 264 (5 new for the KMS cipher, mocking `send` the same way the existing deletion-confirmation journal test does). **Found and fixed along the way:** `drizzle-orm`'s Postgres.js wrapper (used for the shared `database.client` since it also backs Drizzle reads) breaks `postgres.js`'s own raw-`Date`-parameter serialization for any tagged-template query on that client, even one with no other unusual parameters; every other raw insert in this package already avoids it by binding an ISO string with an explicit cast instead of a `Date` object, and the vault now does the same. **Gateway HTTP API built and locally verified (2026-09-28):** three new routes under `/v1/profile/restricted-answers`, added to the same OpenAPI contract and ajv-validated request/response schemas as the rest of Profile - `GET` (list, metadata only), `PUT /{fieldKey}` (create or correct, `Idempotency-Key` required, optimistic `expectedVersion`, `Cache-Control: no-store`), `DELETE /{fieldKey}` (delete-by-field, no version to track - matching `deleteResume`'s idempotent-on-absence convention rather than every other route's version-checked convention, because a delete carries no overwrite risk). The store gained real idempotency-key handling (checked outside any lock before encrypting to skip a pointless KMS call on a retry, then authoritatively inside the transaction) and a `removeByField` convenience so no caller needs to track a version just to delete; both are tested with 9 more real-PostgreSQL cases (28 total in this file), including that reusing one idempotency key for a different non-value field is refused but for a different *value* is deliberately not distinguished (documented and tested: hashing the value would let a row reader brute-force a low-entropy answer without calling KMS). The Lambda boundary allowlist, the exception-to-problem-detail mapping (409 version and idempotency conflicts, 400 invalid input, 503 when unconfigured), and the composition wiring in both `lambda.ts` and `main.ts` (an optional `RESTRICTED_ANSWER_VAULT_KMS_KEY_ARN`; listing and deleting need no key, saving or reading a value fails closed with a clean 503 without one) are all in place. Tests: Profile unit 102 (+7), Profile real-PostgreSQL integration 28 (+9), Gateway unit 277 (+13, including that the plaintext value never appears in a save confirmation or a list response). Repo-wide typecheck, lint (baseline only), and format all pass. **Terraform written and validated 2026-09-28, not applied:** [restricted-answer-vault.tf](../../../infrastructure/terraform/modules/platform/restricted-answer-vault.tf) provisions a genuinely separate KMS key (owner decision, "Provision a genuinely new, dedicated key": the repo's only existing key, `aws_kms_key.platform`, also encrypts the RDS volume itself, so reusing it would not have been separate from the database key as ADR-010 asks and as approved). The gateway Lambda role gets Encrypt/Decrypt scoped to exactly this new key; every resource is behind a new `restricted_answer_vault_enabled` flag, default false, so an environment that has not opted in sees no change at all. `terraform fmt` and `terraform validate` pass in both the development and staging environments. **Development migration deployed 2026-09-29; KMS activation remains dark:** Profile `0010` applied successfully through the scanned, checksum-reviewed migration image. Gateway version 85 contains the vault API, but saving or reading a value still fails closed until the dedicated key and exact role/environment binding are applied. The development and staging roots now expose the flag as default false, and the Gateway operator has a mutually exclusive, saved-plan-only `RestrictedAnswerVault` activation with exact resource, live-key, role-grant, Gateway-boundary, and zero-drift checks. No vault activation plan has been generated or applied. **Not built:** the review panel or a settings page actually offering "save this answer" to a candidate (Applications' "vault-backed proposals" reading `readActiveValue`, per D3-005), and decrypting the value into a candidate data export. **Still blocked on:** candidate-facing save/reuse UI, decrypting restricted values into a candidate export, the dedicated-key activation described above, and the broader D3-012 review before real-candidate restricted answers. D3-R4's key-separation mechanism is decided and built; the review it also named remains D3-012's to close. |

Defaults: 15-minute staleness margin, 50 per sweep, both env-overridable; these are operational tuning values, not security boundaries. 19 new unit tests; the store method itself already has real-PostgreSQL coverage. The artifact builds locally (`pnpm --filter @ai-job-apply/workflows build:lambda:applications-attempt-recovery`). **Deployment design:** the Lambda, role, EventBridge rule, DLQ, and alarms are isolated behind a default-false input and can exist only through the reviewed apply flow. From a reviewed schema-7 zero-drift baseline at state serial 392, the owner-approved activation plan applied exactly 10 creates, one private endpoint-policy update, and zero destroys. Live checks passed: the enabled `rate(5 minutes)` schedule targets the immutable Lambda version with reserved concurrency 1; the 30-minute health window showed two invocations, zero errors, zero throttles, and an empty dead-letter queue. The post-apply plan contains zero meaningful resource or output changes, but standalone verification stopped because six newly created recovery resources exposed only provider normalizations (null-to-empty tags and alarm actions, plus the source-evaluated IAM inline-policy snapshot). This owner-approved guard fix admits only those exact addresses, types, shapes, and planned values; adversarial fixtures and the saved post-apply plan pass locally. No Terraform reapply is needed. After that guard merged, standalone Verify accepted the drift shape but stopped at the retained-baseline state binding: Terraform 1.16.2 wrote two consecutive versioned backend snapshots during the exact apply, serials 393 and 394 one second apart, while the verifier assumed every apply advances serial exactly once. Versioned backend history proves those are the only state-object versions after the reviewed baseline, the final state is serial 394, and the final saved plan remains zero meaningful drift. The owner approved a one-time exception pinned to the exact source, receipt, baseline, baseline-metadata, and activation-plan hashes plus the two exact backend timestamps; 494 adversarial guard assertions and a read-only check against the live version history pass. No Terraform reapply is needed. Standalone verification remains pending until this second guard change is merged. **The outbox publisher is deliberately deferred** (owner decision 2026-09-28, "Build the stale-attempt scheduler now, defer the publisher"): the outbox table already captures `application.applied`/`application.status_changed` durably and correctly with no data loss, and draining it to a bus has no subscriber until D5 (Notifications) exists; building that contract before its consumer means designing it twice. **Terraform design written and validated 2026-09-28; applied in development 2026-09-29:** [applications-attempt-recovery.tf](../../../infrastructure/terraform/modules/platform/applications-attempt-recovery.tf) provisions the scheduler as its own isolated Lambda, role, and `rate(5 minutes)` EventBridge rule (not folded into the profile-readiness activity Lambda, matching this repo's per-service ownership rule), plus error and dead-letter alarms matching the existing profile-readiness pattern. Behind a new `applications_attempt_recovery_enabled` flag, default false. `terraform fmt` and `terraform validate` pass in both environments. **Remaining:** vault-backed proposals, standalone recovery verification after the hash-pinned two-snapshot fix merges, and D3-014. |

Any option is a guard change and needs the owner's explicit approval. **Trigger:** do this before the next Gateway or document release that needs a baseline. The owner approved a narrow fix (2026-09-20, "Approve the narrow fix"): [the guard](../../../scripts/lib/development-gateway-release-guards.ps1) now accepts exactly that one computed update, only with a parser refresh, and rejects any other output change or that one in any other shape (19 new assertions, 297 in all). The corrected guard accepts the real saved plan. Nothing in AWS changed: the document and Gateway artifacts were published as immutable objects and a baseline and plan were written locally. Merging the fix changes the release commit, so the publications, the schema-8 baseline, and the plan are redone at the new commit. **Done 2026-09-20.** The release ran from the schema-8 baseline: a plan of exactly six Lambda function and alias updates (sha256 `2a9fff28...`) plus the one accepted parser-version output change was reviewed by the owner, applied, and verified with zero drift and a passing search boundary. Live aliases now: Gateway 80, document parser 7, document processor 12, all from one document receipt at the release commit. A schema-7 baseline captured from that single receipt (zero drift, state serial 375) proves convergence, so ordinary Gateway releases work again. See the [runbook](../../../infrastructure/runbooks/deployments/development-gateway-release.md#converging-a-mixed-document-release-schema-8-baseline). |

Changing the binding on a guess would trade a suspected fix for double-encoded data, so the first step is diagnostic: the Gateway now logs the PostgreSQL SQLSTATE for this route ([classifier](../../../backend/gateway/src/app.module.ts)), the same regex-validated pattern already used for job contributions. Done when the owner's synthetic upload registers, scans, parses, and shows a tailored resume. **Root cause found 2026-09-20.** The Gateway hands the Documents store its shared Profile client, which installs an identity JSON serializer, so `transaction.json(...)` fails there with `ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE` (the repo's own Applications database note describes the same trap). Local tests used a plain client, where the wrapper works, which is why the 2026-09-01 "repair" back to the wrapper passed locally and broke the deployed path, and why the SQLSTATE log added first showed nothing: this was a driver error, not a database error. **Fix:** registration binds explicit JSON text and casts it in SQL (`::text::jsonb`), which stores structured JSONB on both clients ([store](../../../backend/services/documents/src/index.ts)). **Proof:** a new integration test ([test](../../../backend/services/documents/src/documents.integration.test.ts)) registers and replays an upload on the production-shaped shared client and checks the stored JSONB is an object; on the old code that test fails with exactly `ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE`, with the fix it passes (Documents 121 unit and 12 integration). The route now also logs a fixed-format driver error code ([classifier](../../../backend/gateway/src/app.module.ts)). **Same pattern elsewhere:** the Billing store writes events with `transaction.json` on this shared client; that path is not exercised yet and is a follow-up. **Not done until** the fix is released and the owner's synthetic upload registers, scans, parses, and shows a tailored resume. **Second cause found 2026-09-20:** with registration fixed (Gateway version 82), the browser's file post to S3 failed with "Could not upload the resume. Try again with a new upload grant." The bucket's CORS list allowed only `http://localhost:3000`: S3 returns CORS headers for that origin and none for `https://ai-job-apply-candidate.vercel.app`, so the browser blocks the cross-origin post. The Terraform already takes a list of origins; the value could not be changed because no guarded step could. Owner approved a narrow step: a `-DocumentUploadCorsOrigin` mode that appends exactly one HTTPS origin, from a current baseline, as one in-place update of the CORS configuration ([guards](../../../scripts/lib/development-document-upload-cors-guards.ps1), [operator](../../../scripts/operate-development-gateway-release.ps1), [runbook](../../../infrastructure/runbooks/deployments/development-gateway-release.md#allowing-an-upload-origin-d3-024)); 98 guard assertions and operator boundary tests, and the guard accepts the real Terraform plan shape. Not yet run. **Upload origin applied 2026-09-20** (owner approved the saved plan, sha256 `6f2d0db7...`): the live bucket CORS now lists `http://localhost:3000` and `https://ai-job-apply-candidate.vercel.app` (S3 returns the CORS header for both and none for an unrelated origin). The operator's post-apply verification then stopped: Terraform's S3 bucket resource reads back the CORS rules, so the next plan reported one refresh record on the bucket whose only difference is `cors_rule`, which the shared drift check did not expect (it only knew a policy refresh on that resource). That would also have blocked every later baseline and release. The owner approved a narrow fix (2026-09-20): [the drift check](../../../scripts/lib/development-gateway-release-guards.ps1) now accepts exactly that record (same methods, headers, exposed headers, and max age; origin list equal to the old list with origins appended and to the source-evaluated CORS configuration; nothing else differs), 39 new assertions (350 in all), and it accepts the real failing verification plan. The next apply commits the refreshed state. **Done 2026-09-20 on the owner's confirmation** ("I can now successfully upload resume"): registration and the browser's post to S3 both work in the hosted app. What the owner confirmed is the upload; that the resume is then scanned, parsed, and shown as a tailored resume remains D3-023's own done criterion. |
| D3-025 | Limit stored resumes by plan and block a new upload while one is still being processed. The owner asked for at most one resume per candidate, gated through Billing (no plan exists yet, so it must be plan-driven and support later plans), and for no second upload while a resume is uploading, scanning, or parsing. | Done | Product owner (approval of [ADR-032](../../adr/032-billing-owned-plan-limits-enforced-by-owning-contexts.md)); implementer | D3-023, D3-024 | Design: [ADR-032](../../adr/032-billing-owned-plan-limits-enforced-by-owning-contexts.md) (Proposed). **Billing owns the number, the owning context enforces it.** Billing gains a versioned `planLimits` table and an evaluated `limits.maxStoredResumes` on every valid entitlement (free 1; pro 1 pending a product decision) ([Billing limits](../../../backend/services/billing/src/limits.ts)). The Documents store enforces the limit atomically, inside the registration transaction, under a per-candidate advisory lock, through a `ResumeLimits` port the Gateway composes from Billing ([Documents store](../../../backend/services/documents/src/index.ts), [Gateway port](../../../backend/gateway/src/resume-limits.ts)); a stored resume is one that is uploading, scanning, parsing, or parsed, so a failed one frees the slot. A registration that never uploaded (older than 15 minutes) is failed by the next registration, matching the web's own recovery window, so a closed tab cannot lock the candidate out. The store fails closed: an unavailable or malformed limit refuses the upload. Refusals are stable 409 problems `resume_upload_in_progress` and `resume_document_limit`, and `EntitlementSummary` gains an additive `limits` object (OpenAPI regenerated). The web disables the picker and submit and shows a fixed reason in a status region while a resume is in flight or the plan's limit is reached, and shows fixed copy if the server refuses ([upload form](../../../frontend/web/app/resume-upload-form.tsx)). **Local evidence only, not deployed:** Billing 44 unit and 8 integration; Documents 130 unit and 17 integration, including a real-PostgreSQL race (6 candidates, 2 concurrent registrations each, exactly one wins) and a lifecycle test; Gateway 243; web 712; contracts 70; typecheck 37 of 37; Biome clean. A real-Chromium run of the form (40 checks, 0 failures) covered empty, uploading, scanning, parsing, parsed at limit 1 and 2, failed-only, abandoned, limit-unknown, server refusal with fixed copy and no storage post, and Tab/status-region behaviour. **Not done until** the Gateway is released and the web redeployed and the owner sees a second upload refused with a resume stored or in flight. **Known gaps:** there is no per-resume delete or replace action, so with a limit of 1 a parsed resume cannot be swapped (recommend a follow-up task); Pro equals Free until a product decision; the Billing store still writes events with `transaction.json` on the shared client (same trap as D3-024, not exercised yet); replaying an earlier registration returns 503 if Billing is unavailable because the limit is resolved first. **Deployed and Done 2026-09-21 on the owner's hosted confirmation** ("works", after the four-step check: limit note with a resume stored, upload disabled while a resume is processing, delete then reupload, limit note again once parsed). Released with D3-026 below: Gateway version 83, web production deployment `ai-job-apply-candidate-gy7sy76xj`. |
| D3-026 | Let a candidate delete a stored resume so the one-resume limit does not trap them (owner: "the approach is delete then reupload new one"). Owner chose to remove the resume now and purge the stored bytes in a follow-up (D3-027). | Done | Product owner (approval of [ADR-033](../../adr/033-resume-deletion-is-a-tombstone-with-a-separate-byte-purge.md)); implementer | D3-025 | Design: [ADR-033](../../adr/033-resume-deletion-is-a-tombstone-with-a-separate-byte-purge.md) (Proposed). **Tombstone, not byte erasure.** Migration `0004_resume_deletion` adds `deleted_at`; only `parsed`, `failed`, or `rejected` resumes can be deleted, it can never be cleared, and the row and object keys stay so candidate data deletion still purges the bytes ([migration](../../../backend/services/documents/migrations/0004_resume_deletion.sql)). `DELETE /v1/resumes/{documentId}` (`documents:write`, idempotent, owner-scoped, 204; 409 `resume_not_deletable` while processing) hides the resume, frees the plan slot under the per-candidate lock, and removes it from tailoring ([store](../../../backend/services/documents/src/index.ts)). The web form shows Delete only for finished resumes, asks for confirmation ("Facts already extracted from it stay in your profile"), moves focus deliberately, and shows fixed copy on refusal or timeout ([form](../../../frontend/web/app/resume-upload-form.tsx)). **Guard and pin edits, each owner-approved 2026-09-20:** the migration inventory pins went from 48 to 49 (restore manifest and checksum, image publisher smoke lists, Dockerfile smoke, staging restore verifier, D2 runtime release count, and their tests); nothing else in those files changed. **Local evidence only, not deployed:** Documents 276 unit-run and 21 integration (real PostgreSQL: delete then upload, idempotent repeat with one audit event, other candidate cannot delete, tombstone kept in the object inventory, tailoring lookup ignores it, database refuses un-delete and deleting a processing resume, delete versus concurrent uploads); Gateway app and Lambda allowlist tests for the route, scope, id shape, query, and 409; web 725; contracts 70; typecheck 37 of 37; `pnpm infrastructure:validate` passes. A real-Chromium run (16 checks, 0 failed) covered keyboard delete, cancel, confirm, focus, announcement, refusal copy with no upstream text, and no delete control for a resume still processing. **Not done until** the migration image is published and Documents 0004 applied, the Gateway released, and the web redeployed through their guarded operators (each plan owner-reviewed), and the owner deletes a resume and uploads another in the hosted app. **Known gaps:** stored bytes remain until D3-027; unreviewed Profile proposals from a deleted resume remain until then; the candidate export still lists the tombstone. **Release progress 2026-09-20:** the migration image for `69f1068` was published and verified (two identical deterministic builds, ECR scan complete with 0 critical and 0 high). A fresh zero-drift schema-7 baseline captured (state serial 380). `PlanTask` then stopped: the migration operator's own [document drift check](../../../scripts/lib/development-document-dark-guards.ps1) did not know the bucket CORS refresh record left by the D3-024 origin step (the Gateway checks learned it in #429). The record was confirmed to differ only in `cors_rule`. **Owner approved a narrow fix** (2026-09-20): that check now sends a bucket record that changes `cors_rule` through the same reviewed CORS check the Gateway release uses and leaves every other record and bucket change to the unchanged checks; new tests cover accepting it, the appended-origin, planned-list, rule attribute, policy, sensitive, unknown, replace-path, and duplicate rejections, and the fix was shown to fail without it and to accept the real captured plan. Because operators bind the image and plans to the merged commit, the image is republished after this merges. **Deployed and Done 2026-09-21 on the owner's hosted confirmation** ("works"). Release evidence, every step through its guarded operator with the owner reviewing the saved plan or descriptor first: (1) migration image for merge `6be9ea9`, receipt sha256 `081d94ec...`, two identical builds, ECR scan 0 critical and 0 high (the first image, for `69f1068`, was superseded because operators bind to the merged commit); (2) migration task definition revision 58, plan sha256 `65034c7d...`, only `aws_ecs_task_definition.migration[0]`; (3) one execution, descriptor sha256 `ce177073...`, exit 0 with all 8 aggregate success messages (Documents through `0004_resume_deletion`), Verify with a zero-drift plan; (4) Gateway version 83, plan sha256 `d53d83bb...`, exactly the function and alias updates, applied and verified with zero drift and the document scan triggers still on; (5) web production deploy after `Validate` and `Preflight` and a pinned dry run (151 files, credentials excluded); unauthenticated smoke of the new BFF route: 401 for a valid id, 400 for a malformed id. The owner then deleted a resume and uploaded another in the hosted app. The guard fix that unblocked the migration operator (bucket CORS refresh record in the document drift check) is #432. **Still open:** stored bytes of deleted resumes remain until D3-027; ADR-032 and ADR-033 are Proposed. |
| D3-027 | Purge the stored bytes of deleted resumes (the second half of ADR-033): every version and delete marker under the resume's source and normalized prefixes is removed and verified gone, then the Documents row, its idempotency entries (which hold the file name), receipts, and dispositions, and the unreviewed Profile proposals derived only from it. Needed before ADR-023's real-resume gates can be reviewed. | Done | Product owner (decisions and approvals); implementer | D3-026 | **Done 2026-09-28 on the owner's confirmation; the history below is in order, so its early sentences describe the state at the time.** *(Was: designed and approved 2026-09-21, nothing built.)* [design](../../security/d3-027-resume-byte-purge-design.md) and [ADR-034](../../adr/034-resume-byte-purge-runs-in-the-candidate-rights-maintenance-runtime.md) (Proposed). Proposal: a second step of the existing `rate(5 minutes)` candidate-rights maintenance handler, in the role that already holds object-version delete, so no IAM, Lambda, schedule, or alarm resource is added; keys derived from the document id and validated; second listing must be empty before any database removal; Documents-owned SQL functions (migration Documents `0005`, pins 49 to 50) and a Profile-owned store method (no Profile migration); the Gateway gets no delete right. Slices: (1) migrations and functions with real-PostgreSQL tests, (2) migration image and execution, (3) sweep and a narrow guarded release mode for the activity Lambda's code, (4) immediate proposals retirement in the delete route and the confirmation copy, (5) deployed synthetic acceptance. **Owner approved all three recommendations 2026-09-21:** erase unreviewed proposals when their only source resume is purged, keep security-held bytes 30 days then purge, and this mechanism (a dedicated purge Lambda stays the later hardening). Guard edits and saved plans still need their own approvals. **Not done until** the sweep is released and a synthetic delete is shown to leave no version, marker, row, or proposal. **Slice 1 built locally 2026-09-21, not merged or deployed:** Documents migration `0005_resume_purge` with `pending_resume_purges` (identifiers only, oldest first, security-held tombstones only after 30 days, bounded batch) and `complete_resume_purge` (requires the objects-absent attestation, takes the per-candidate lock, removes the row, the idempotency entries that name the document including a string-encoded response, receipts and dispositions, and writes one value-free `resume.purged` event; a second call is a no-op), and the Profile-owned `PostgresResumeProposalWriter.retireDocumentProposals` (erases only facts whose every version is still `proposed` and whose only source is the document, keeps confirmed, edited, and rejected facts and mixed-source facts, removes the import record, idempotent). This replaced the designed Profile SQL function so Profile needs no migration. Real-PostgreSQL tests: Documents 25 integration (4 new: readiness and ordering, complete removal with the file name, refusals, ordering against an upload), Profile 10 integration (1 new). Migration inventory pins moved from 49 to 50 with the owner's approval (2026-09-21), mechanical only. **Slice 1 deployed 2026-09-21:** merged as #435; migration image for `45390b3` (receipt sha256 `fb672ad8...`, two identical builds, 0 critical and 0 high), task definition revision 59 (plan sha256 `82da01a6...`, image change only), one execution (descriptor sha256 `9c2eebb2...`, exit 0, all 8 aggregate messages, Documents through `0005_resume_purge`), Verify with a zero-drift plan, each step with the owner reviewing the saved plan or descriptor. Nothing calls the new functions yet. **Slice 3 built locally 2026-09-21, not merged or deployed:** (a) the sweep: `AwsCandidateDocumentObjectStore.purgeDocumentPrefixes` (prefixes derived from a validated document id, every version and marker under `quarantine/<id>/` and `normalized/<id>/` including an older parser version, a second listing that reports what remains) and `PostgresCandidateRightsActivityStore.purgeDeletedResumes` (retire proposals, purge objects, only if none remain call `complete_resume_purge`; one failing tombstone does not stop the others; counts-only logging), wired as a second step of the maintenance handler that runs both sweeps and then raises on any failure so the error and dead-letter alarms fire; tests: workflows 1264 unit, plus 2 real-PostgreSQL end-to-end tests of the sweep with the real Documents and Profile functions. (b) the guarded release mode `-RefreshCandidateRightsActivity` ([guards](../../../scripts/lib/development-candidate-rights-activity-guards.ps1), [runbook](../../../infrastructure/runbooks/deployments/development-gateway-release.md#refreshing-the-candidate-rights-activity-code-d3-027)): an exploratory read-only plan showed a code-only refresh changes 12 resources and 2 outputs (the archive also serves the terminal Lambda and the module binds the activity version into the durable workflow), and the owner approved exactly that set; the guard requires only the four activity artifact variables to change and to equal the verified receipt, each resource to differ only in its reviewed attributes, no other resource or output, and after apply it reads the live chain back and requires zero drift. 93 fixture assertions (every resource, action, replacement, attribute, unknown, sensitive, output, receipt, and variable mutation refused) and the guard accepts the real exploratory plan; operator boundary tests pass. **Still to do:** merge, publish the workflow artifacts, plan and review the release, apply and verify, then slice 4 (immediate proposal retirement in the delete route and the confirmation copy) and slice 5 (deployed synthetic acceptance). **Slice 3 deployed 2026-09-21, purge not yet working:** merged as #436; workflow artifacts for `eac23a4` published and verified (receipt sha256 `775490d8...`, 12 objects, two identical builds), a fresh baseline captured, and the reviewed plan (sha256 `b0339bdd...`, exactly the 12 approved resources and 2 outputs) applied through `-RefreshCandidateRightsActivity`: the activity, terminal, durable, and outbox-starter aliases are now version 16 and the operator's live chain checks passed (both Lambdas on the reviewed archive, the durable version invoking the new activity version, the terminal Lambda, outbox starter, and terminal rule naming the new durable version, the durable role invoking exactly that version). The operator's last step, the zero-drift plan, stopped on two refresh records the shared drift check did not know: the outbox-starter and durable IAM roles report their inline policy snapshot as stale after the reviewed policy change (one statement each, Lambda version 15 to 16, nothing else); the plan itself had zero resource changes. The owner approved a narrow fix (2026-09-21) in the [shared drift check](../../../scripts/lib/development-gateway-release-guards.ps1): exactly those two records, only when the new policy equals the source-evaluated policy and is the old one with the one named statement advanced to a higher version of the same function; 80 new fixture assertions, and it accepts the real post-apply plan. **The first live sweep then found 2 tombstones (the owner's earlier hosted delete tests) and both purges failed** with only a generic code, so the [sweep](../../../backend/workflows/src/candidate-rights-activities.ts) now logs the failed step (retire proposals, purge objects, complete purge) plus fixed-format fields (error name, SQLSTATE, AWS code, HTTP status; never a message, key, or identifier), the same approach that found the registration fault in D3-024. Database rows are untouched by a failed purge. **Next:** merge, publish, plan and apply the diagnostics release, read the failure from the log, fix it. **Diagnostics release blocked by a guard gap, fixed 2026-09-21:** with #437 merged, the workflow artifacts for `bd31586` were published and verified (receipt sha256 `f9f34b34...`) and a baseline captured, but planning the diagnostics release stopped: that plan rewrites the same two role policies again (a new activity version), so their planned text is unknown and the new role-refresh check crashed on the absent attribute instead of deciding. The owner approved a narrow relaxation (2026-09-21): when the planned text is absent the refresh record is accepted only if the same plan updates that exact role policy in place with the policy unknown, with every other condition unchanged (one named statement advanced to a higher version of the same function, nothing else differs); any other absence is refused cleanly. 24 new fixture assertions (450 in the suite) and it accepts the real failing plan. **Cause of the failed purges found 2026-09-21:** the diagnostics release (activity archive `d2e8ddf6...`, plan sha256 `f94eefcc...`, applied and verified with zero drift, the two role refresh records accepted and the state refreshed) logged `step: purge_objects`, error name `Error`, i.e. my own guard on the S3 delete response, not an AWS exception. Read-only checks then showed no Object Lock, the five uploaded objects and three normalized objects present, and that the **workload permissions boundary allows `s3:DeleteObjectVersion` only on the candidate-exports bucket** (`OperateCandidateDataExportObjects`), never on `candidate-docs-*/*`, so S3 denied every DeleteObjects on documents whatever the role policy and endpoint policy allow. The owner approved one boundary statement (2026-09-21), `PurgeDeletedCandidateDocumentVersions`: `s3:DeleteObjectVersion` on `candidate-docs-*/*` only when `aws:PrincipalArn` is the activity role; the bootstrap operator's change set is now `candidate-documents-version-delete-v1` (exact added statement, previous change set retained and verified), with 30 fixture assertions refusing every wider variant. Applying it is a separate, owner-reviewed bootstrap plan. See [boundary runbook](../../security/bootstrap-workload-boundary-runbook.md). **Boundary fix applied and the purge works in development, 2026-09-21:** #439 merged (the operator and Terraform pin the exact statement and the bootstrap operator's change set `candidate-documents-version-delete-v1`); the owner reviewed the saved bootstrap plan (sha256 `6008e2f2...`) and it was applied through `operate-bootstrap-workload-boundary.ps1`, adding the one statement and nothing else (staging's boundary is unchanged). The next 5-minute sweep purged both tombstones from the owner's earlier hosted delete tests: `resume_purge_swept` with `purged: 2, failed: 0`, the source prefix went from 5 objects to 3 and the normalized prefix from 3 to 1 (the remaining objects belong to the live resume), and no delete marker was left. The database side (row, idempotency entries, receipts, `resume.purged` audit) is not directly readable from outside the runtime; it is proved by the sweep's `purged` count, which is only counted after `complete_resume_purge` returns, and by the real-PostgreSQL tests. **Slice 4 built locally 2026-09-21, not merged or deployed:** the delete route now also retires the candidate's unreviewed proposals from that resume at once, so the review list is clean without waiting for the sweep: a new `ProfileStore.retireResumeProposals(userId, documentId, traceId)` (owner-scoped, one transaction over the Profile-owned `retireDocumentProposals`; the caller's identity comes from the token, never the request), called by the Gateway after `documents.deleteResume` succeeds. It is best effort: a failure logs the fixed event `resume_proposal_retirement_deferred` (no message or identifier) and the request still answers 204, because the resume is already deleted and the sweep repeats the erase before it purges. It is not called when the delete is refused. The web confirmation now reads "Facts you already reviewed stay in your profile. Suggestions you have not reviewed are removed." and the page refreshes the review list after a delete. Tests: Gateway 254 unit (3 new: retire on success for that owner and document, none when the delete is refused, 204 with the deferred log and no leaked text when retirement fails), web 726 (1 new: the callback fires once after a delete and never on a refusal; the copy test updated), Profile 11 real-PostgreSQL (1 new: the store method erases only the caller's proposals and is idempotent). **Still to do:** merge, publish and release the Gateway, deploy the web app, then slice 5 (deployed synthetic acceptance including account deletion of a candidate who has a document). **Slice 4 released and the deployed acceptance passed, 2026-09-28:** #440 merged (`d78903e`); the Gateway artifact for it was published and verified (receipt sha256 `1c39b130...`), a schema-7 baseline captured from the last zero-drift plan, and the reviewed plan (sha256 `749b1d87...`, two updates: the Gateway function code and its alias, no IAM, network, trigger, or output change) applied and verified with zero drift (verification plan sha256 `78ca6bd0...`); the candidate web app was deployed to production after Validate, Preflight, and a dry-run whose 151-file manifest stayed inside the approved upload boundary (deployment `ai-job-apply-candidate-g6szhvak2`, Ready, hosted origin 200). **Acceptance in the hosted app by the owner, checked read-only:** the owner deleted the stored resume (`01a0c47d...`), uploaded a new one (`01a0e93d...`, parsed within 5 seconds), and deleted that too, confirming the new confirmation copy and that the unreviewed suggestions disappeared at once while the reviewed fact stayed. The next sweep (2026-09-28 18:22 UTC) logged `scanned: 2, purged: 2, failed: 0, heldWaiting: 0`; afterwards neither document had any version under `quarantine/` or `normalized/`, the bucket held no delete marker, and no `resume_proposal_retirement_deferred` event was logged. An IAM simulation showed the activity role allowed `s3:DeleteObjectVersion` on the documents bucket, with the boundary allowing it, and the Gateway role still implicitly denied. Not directly readable from outside the runtime: the Documents row, idempotency entries, receipts, and the `resume.purged` audit event; they rest on the sweep's `purged` count (counted only after `complete_resume_purge` returns) and the real-PostgreSQL tests. **Not covered, tracked separately:** account deletion of a candidate who has a stored resume (D3-R9, now D3-028) and the unexplained older objects (D3-R8). Security-held tombstones (30-day wait) were not exercised deployed; they are covered by the real-PostgreSQL and unit tests. |

## Exit criteria


2026-09-30 D3-001 implementation delta: D3-030 builds the separately gated saved-value export
path described in ADR-035. The default-off source and local tests do not close export activation,
deployed acceptance, Applications reuse, or D3-012. D1 and D3 remain In progress.

- [ ] Every generated claim is grounded in confirmed profile evidence or explicitly requires review.
- [ ] Protected, voluntary, legal, signature, authorization, and other sensitive answers follow mandatory-review policy.
- [ ] Rendered documents preserve intended content and pass format/factuality checks.
- [ ] Generated cover letters and improved/tailored resumes are never persisted and all platform temporary copies expire within 30 minutes; expired material cannot be previewed, downloaded, approved, or handed to an ATS (D3-064).
- [ ] Approval is bound to an immutable packet and expires on material job/form/artifact changes.
- [ ] Supported ATS variants meet agreed field accuracy and confirmation thresholds.
- [ ] Ambiguous submission responses cannot cause blind duplicate submission.
- [ ] Owner-scoped current-action and technical-history reads are authorized, durable, observable,
      recoverable, and end at confirmed submission with `applied` terminal.
- [ ] No recruiter response, interview, offer, rejection, or other post-submission hiring outcome is
      collected or inferred.
- [ ] Unsupported variants degrade to safe assisted/manual completion.
- [ ] User correction, adapter failure, model quality, and confirmation metrics are observable by version.
- [ ] Security, privacy, accessibility, AI evaluation, workflow replay, and product acceptance evidence is complete.
- [ ] Every enabled application adapter has current authorized provider fixtures, sanitization and scan evidence, and separate QA representativeness acceptance.

## Exit-criteria status (updated 2026-09-18; no box is ticked)

A box is ticked only when its whole wording has linked, inspected evidence. Local synthetic evidence below is not deployed, real-data, or approved evidence.

| # | Criterion | Local evidence | What is still missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Generated claims grounded or reviewed | Fact graph, ai-sdk unsupported-claim checks, always-reviewed `generated` fields | Wiring generation into packet preparation; real-model evaluation (D3-012) |
| 2 | Sensitive answers follow mandatory review | Field policy, packet approval requiring acknowledgement, review UI | Vault persistence and separate key (D3-015, D3-R4); counsel/security disposition |
| 3 | Rendered documents preserve content and pass checks | Re-parse verification for DOCX and PDF, verbatim tailoring | Deploying the connection of tailoring to Prepare (D3-016, code done and locally verified), real-resume behavior, human quality review |
| 4 | Approval bound to an immutable packet and expires on change | Packet hash, approval binding, expiry, invalidation on start | API-level synthetic acceptance passed in development 2026-09-19 with no resume, cover letter, or extension in the path (wrong-hash approval refused, changed form invalidates approval); the changed-posting case stays covered by local integration tests; real-token and browser proof still open |
| 5 | Supported ATS variants meet agreed thresholds | Threshold proposal in the fixture request | D3-013 fixtures, adapters, agreed thresholds, measurement |
| 6 | Ambiguous responses cannot cause blind duplicate submission | DB guard, write-ahead dispatch, crash recovery, concurrency race tests | Extension executor and live confirmation detection |
| 7 | Owner-scoped reads authorized, durable, observable, recoverable, ending at `applied` | Store, API, gateway routes, history, metrics | Deployed to development (schema and Gateway, 2026-09-18); API-level synthetic lifecycle acceptance passed 2026-09-19 (24/24 steps, exact cleanup; no resume, cover letter, or extension in the path); real-token sign-in verified 2026-09-19 (create 201, packet 201, hosted web enabled on the protected Production target); alarms, restore, and candidate-rights activation still open (D3-014, D3-015) |
| 8 | No post-submission hiring outcome collected or inferred | Closed history vocabulary, contract/event/UI guards | Repository-wide and deployed confirmation at exit review |
| 9 | Unsupported variants degrade to safe assisted/manual completion | Assisted-manual planner | Extension integration and real-browser test |
| 10 | Correction, adapter failure, model quality, confirmation metrics observable by version | Versioned metrics and rate functions | Production sink, dashboards, thresholds |
| 11 | Security, privacy, accessibility, AI evaluation, workflow replay, product acceptance evidence | Partial (D3-011) | Independent review, workflow replay, real-browser/AT, product acceptance |
| 12 | Every enabled adapter has authorized provider fixtures and QA acceptance | Registry gate that rejects anything else | D3-013 |

| 2026-09-19 | **Real-token Applications check passed in development (owner sign-in, synthetic-safe).** The candidate web project `ai-job-apply-candidate` was deployed from clean `main` (`fafab24`, deployment `dpl_3WQk4EUCCPQpECHyBGP9v6SyYe4a`, `iad1`) with `AUTH0_APPLICATIONS_SCOPES=enabled` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_APPLICATIONS_UI=enabled` set on Production only; the deployed login redirect requested `applications:read applications:write` on audience `ai-job-apply-api`. After the owner's fresh sign-in, the review workspace appeared under a job's details and **Prepare application** produced `POST /v1/applications` 201 (149 ms) and `POST /v1/applications/{id}/packets` 201 (82 ms) in the Gateway log (release `9a9912dd...`, alias version 78); request log lines carry only method, path, status, and timing. Nothing was approved or submitted. **Findings:** (1) a first attempt returned 403 in 1-4 ms because the token lacked `applications:write`; the Auth0 permissions had been defined but the token did not carry them. (2) Enabling **RBAC** with permissions in the access token on the API made the token carry only the permissions assigned to the user (none), so the six existing scopes vanished and API Gateway's `$default` JWT authorizer rejected every call before the Lambda; the web app displays that unexpected upstream reply as a generic 503 ("temporarily unavailable"). Turning RBAC back off, as the Auth0 runbook already requires, restored the scopes. (3) The Gateway Lambda has reserved concurrency 2 (Phase 0 cost control), and the web page fires five parallel calls on load, so a cold start can throttle calls and show the same 503 until they are retried; a product decision on that cap is open. One application with one prepared packet (0 fields, no resume, no profile facts) now exists for the owner's development account and cannot be removed by the candidate-rights path until the d3 policy is deployed. ## Decisions and scope changes

2026-10-04 owner decision: defer hosted CI/CD until production launch; continue backend source work with local verification. This does not waive production dependency/security, deployment, privacy or acceptance gates. #531 merges source-only as 7cf19d0; complete tree 77c82be matches locally verified exact head 6c3a1ab. Hosted integration/containers were not started because of GitHub payment/spending limits and are not claimed verified. No deployment or activation.

2026-10-03 D3-046 scope decision: after the separately scoped request, the owner instructed
engineering not to ask for approvals and to proceed with recommendations. This authorizes the
recommended source-only Documents durable response inventory, private ledger API, explicit
forward migration and affected strict guard/fixture updates. It does not authorize public APIs,
frontend changes, Terraform, live cloud/model calls, deployment, apply or activation. There is
no backfill of guessed response identities. Apply `0008` before composing compatible private
actors; keep all generation gates off during rollout. Older 57-migration evidence remains valid
only against its own source boundary, never as current 58-migration acceptance. Unknown creation
acknowledgements remain unresolved; disposal evidence does not settle writers or release purge.

2026-10-03 D3-045 scope decision: the owner approved source-only provider-stop/retention work,
then separately approved background execution's temporary provider-response retention tradeoff.
OpenAI describes roughly ten minutes of temporary polling state even with `store:false`, not
an exact maximum or removal of abuse-monitoring copies. No live-call, real-data, production,
deployment, activation, Terraform/guard or migration authority is implied. The old foreground
adapter remains unchanged. Unknown original identities and missing stopping/deletion receipts
must remain unresolved; neither elapsed time nor provider 404 permits platform purge. Durable
identity capture, original gateway dispatch, private writer drain and runtime authentication
must precede composition. A response cleanup receipt alone cannot satisfy the existing
generation-quiescence authority.

2026-10-03 D3-044 scope decision: the owner approved source-only internal non-dispatch receipts
and private phase cleanup only. The receipt must originate in a known gateway refusal before
provider invocation, not a caught error. Private v1 `settle_undispatched` is additive and refused
by older handlers; matching control must precede actor rollout. No public API/frontend, migration,
guard/Terraform, cloud/model calls, deployment, quiescence certification or purge is approved.

2026-10-03 D3-043 scope decision: owner approval covers source-only internal positive completion
receipts and private provider-phase cleanup. It does not authorize clearing failed/ambiguous remote
calls, object purge, production authentication/IAM, migration, public API/frontend changes,
operational guard/Terraform edits, live model/cloud calls or deployment. The existing private v1
control adds `settle_rejected`; older compositions refuse it rather than treating it as ordinary
settlement. Any rollout must compose the matching reviewed control first, while remaining dark.
The new receipt is original-port completion evidence only, not a production quiescence certificate.

2026-10-03 D3-042 scope decision: the owner's "approved" authorizes a source-only private cleanup
API, excluding public APIs/frontend, Terraform and deployment. The safe first cleanup step is an
exact-reference permanent admission fence using the existing ledger, not timeout-based settlement
or object deletion. A separate maintenance capability leaves provider control unchanged. Running
actors still require independent trusted stopping/completion evidence; a terminal ledger receipt
cannot release the existing candidate-rights quiescence gate. No migration/guard change is needed.

2026-10-02 D3-039 scope decision: the owner's "approved" accepts the scoped Profile concurrency
fix only, not the separate runtime-pin/guard proposal. Existing owner isolation, version-conflict
semantics, append-only history and atomic idempotency/audit remain. No new routes/fields, migration,
frontend, Terraform/guard, deployment or apply are authorized by this approval.
[Code](../../../backend/services/profile/src/index.ts),
[test](../../../backend/services/profile/src/profile.integration.test.ts).

| 2026-09-19 | **Owner approved the Profile fact-key convention** ("Approve as proposed") and it is implemented: 26 exact `kind/key` rows fill the packet's automatic fields, namely `identity` facts for legal and preferred name, email, phone, address, and links; `employment` for current company and title and start and end dates; `education` for school, degree, field, and graduation date; and `preference` for notice period, available start date, salary expectation, relocation, and remote preference. A field is filled only from exactly one confirmed, currently usable fact whose value fits the field's type (ISO calendar dates, finite non-negative decimals, booleans, non-empty bounded text; arrays and other shapes are left to the candidate); competing facts choose none; the candidate's own entry wins. Restricted answers (authorization, disclosures, demographics, attestations, signatures), narrative questions, and documents are never mapped. Restore replay always purges Applications data. The D3 engineering policy is recorded with selection authority `product-owner-phase-3-directive` (the owner's instruction to implement Phase 3), grants generated-synthetic development use only, and leaves real-candidate use, public policy, accountable approval, and legal approval ungranted; **the owner should confirm this selection** before it is activated. | An in-place switch would make every guarded operator refuse the deployed seven-owner variables until a coordinated rollout; the dual inventory decouples them and keeps the deployed policy valid. | [retention and deletion](../../security/retention-and-deletion.md) |
| 2026-09-18 | Owner statements recorded verbatim in chat on 2026-09-18: **ADR-030 and the engineering defaults: "accept"**; **D3-012 sign-offs: "approved"**; **D3-013 sources and QA acceptance: "authorized"**. ADR-030 is now Accepted for the gateway, grounding and review architecture; the defaults (24-hour approval, 0.90 review threshold, one live application per job, duplicate-risk acknowledgement, provenance confidence, 15-minute AI disclosure) are product-accepted. The fact-key mapping request was not answered concretely then; the owner approved a convention on 2026-09-19 (see that date's decision row). The D3-012 and D3-013 statements are recorded as the product owner's, not as an attributable counsel or security/privacy disposition or as accepted provider fixtures: the legal review register still requires named reviewers, scope, and reviewed artifact commits, and no fixture has been captured, sanitized, or QA-accepted. No code gate changed: no provider adapter exists, so no live ATS effect is possible, and no real model is connected. | The register and ADR-021 require attributable records and real evidence; an owner statement cannot substitute for them. | [ADR-030](../../adr/030-ai-gateway-and-grounded-generation.md), [approval packet](../../security/d3-application-approval-packet.md), [fixture request](../foundation/d3-provider-fixture-request.md) |
| 2026-09-18 | The owner authorized cloud deployment to the development account and told the agent to rework anything necessary. Recorded as: guarded operators only, and the pinned migration/restore inventories may be updated to add Applications (D3-015). The owner chose a pull request (agent pushes a branch and opens it; the owner reviews and merges) as the route to `origin/main`. No Terraform route change is planned: Applications tokens must also carry an existing route-authorizer scope, and the Gateway enforces the exact `applications:*` scope. Candidate export/deletion, outbox publishing, and stale-attempt scheduling are deliberately absent from this rollout. | Keeps the deployment inside the reviewed operator chain and the smallest reversible change set. | [Rollout runbook](../../../infrastructure/runbooks/deployments/d3-applications-development-rollout.md) |
| 2026-09-18 | Encode the human gates in code: an adapter never performs a page action unless its kill switch is on, live ATS effects are approved, and unexpired QA-accepted provider-derived fixture evidence covers the exact variant; otherwise it can only suggest values. The three provider adapters ship declared, off, and with no variants. The generic assisted-manual fallback performs no page action and never permits automated submission. | Makes D3-012 and D3-013 enforceable rather than procedural, and keeps synthetic fixtures from ever satisfying provider evidence. | [Registry](../../../packages/ats-sdk/src/registry.ts) |
| 2026-09-18 | Propose ADR-030 (product-owner acceptance pending): one AI gateway, generation dark by default, just-in-time disclosure approval, confirmed non-restricted facts only, strict cited output, whole-draft rejection on any unsupported-claim finding, and always-reviewed generated fields. Provider and model selection are not made. | Generated claims must be checkable and a candidate must never receive an unsupported or sensitive draft. | [ADR-030](../../adr/030-ai-gateway-and-grounded-generation.md) |
| 2026-09-18 | Deterministic tailoring is selection and ordering of verbatim resume bullets by job-term overlap; wording changes are only allowed through grounded generation (D3-004). Entries, headings, dates and employers are never reordered or dropped, and a bullet can never move to another entry. Rendering support is limited to what re-parses identically: PDF uses the standard Latin font and refuses other scripts; DOCX carries Unicode. `yazl` moved from a dev to a runtime dependency of the Documents service. | Keeps every tailored claim traceable to the candidate's own words and makes 'preserves intended content' a mechanical check. | [Tailoring](../../../backend/services/documents/src/tailoring/tailor.ts) |
| 2026-09-18 | Fact confidence is deterministic by provenance (candidate-typed 1.00, resume/import with spans 0.95, without spans 0.90, derived 0.85) and applies only to candidate-confirmed facts; derived facts fall below the 0.90 review threshold so they are always reviewed. Vault answers match only the exact field key and exact recorded context; the vault refuses attestations, signatures, CAPTCHA and MFA values. | Confidence must be reproducible and never a model guess; the vault must not turn a similar question into a silent answer. | [Fact graph](../../../packages/domain/src/fact-graph.ts), [ADR-010](../../adr/010-restricted-answer-vault-and-mandatory-review.md) |
| 2026-09-18 | Approval evidence is the Applications approval record itself, not a Profile consent reference; the API accepts no consent identifier. Candidate-reported confirmation may only carry provider-observed evidence kinds, and `user_attested` evidence is reserved for the reconciliation route. The browser extension reports results, so a client-reported confirmation is treated as candidate-observed evidence, not provider truth. | Keeps one immutable approval per packet hash with a single writer and prevents a client from fabricating stronger evidence than it observed. LR-F04 review may require a separate Profile consent ledger. | [OpenAPI](../../../packages/api-contracts/openapi/platform.v1.json) |
| 2026-09-18 | Fixed engineering defaults for D3-005, all subject to product/security review under D3-012: a live approval expires after 24 hours; the auto-review confidence threshold is 0.90; one live application per candidate and job (a cancelled one may restart); cancellation is impossible once submission is in flight; a candidate-attested "not submitted" reconciliation requires an explicit duplicate-risk acknowledgement; `user_attested` confirmation is recorded as its own evidence kind. | These are conservative product-safety defaults that keep approval scarce and duplicate submission structurally hard; they are not legal or approval conclusions. | [Packet](../../../backend/services/applications/src/packet.ts), [attempts](../../../backend/services/applications/src/attempt.ts) |
| 2026-08-17 | Own authoritative technical application history through confirmed submission in D3 and expose it through owner-scoped Applications reads. | The Applications state machine, packets, attempts, submission evidence, confirmation, and history require one owner and one migration lineage; a Notifications projection cannot authorize or prove these transitions. | [ADR-024](../../adr/024-application-history-and-inbox-phase-ownership.md) |
| 2026-08-12 | Provider-derived form fixture evidence is acquired and accepted with each concrete application adapter. | Form fidelity and representative coverage depend on the declared supported variant. | [ADR-021](../../adr/021-own-provider-fixtures-with-adapter-deliveries.md) |

## Risks and blockers

| ID | State | Risk or blocker | Owner | Next action |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D3-R1 | Open | Model-generated unsupported claims could materially harm candidates. | Unassigned | Provenance, deterministic unsupported-claim checks, and adversarial offline tests exist (D3-004). Real-model factuality evaluation on representative data is still required before any generation UX is enabled. |
| D3-R2 | Open | ATS layout changes can create incorrect values or ambiguous submissions. | Unassigned | Require provider fixtures, canaries, kill switches, packet revalidation, and confirmation reconciliation. |
| D3-R3 | Open | A “one click” promise can obscure mandatory user decisions. | Unassigned | Design review by exception and explicit sensitive/irreversible-action gates. |
| D3-R4 | Mechanism decided and built 2026-09-28; real-candidate use stays blocked on D3-012 | Packet fields can carry Restricted answer values (ADR-010) but the Applications schema currently relies on database-level encryption only; ADR-010 requires a separate key and least-privilege boundary for Restricted values. | Product owner (approved the design); implementer | A dedicated KMS key and application-layer encrypt/decrypt, bound to owner/field/context, are built in D3-001 (not yet deployed). D3-012's broader review of real-candidate restricted-answer use is still open. |
| D3-R5 | Mitigated | Candidate export, deletion, and restore replay for Applications are implemented and locally verified but not activated or deployed; synthetic candidates deleted before activation leave orphaned Applications rows, and a restore replay fails closed on them until purged. | Unassigned | Complete D3-014 before any real-candidate use. |
| D3-R6 | Open | Guarded migration/restore tooling pins the exact seven-owner inventory. Owner approved the source-only 57-migration/83-relation update on 2026-10-02 and the publisher inventory correction on 2026-10-03. D3-041 now aligns the five missing SQL names, binds every name/checksum to the validated manifest and passes 149 executable regressions on both PowerShell hosts plus the hardened retained-image smoke. Explicit owner SQL cache inputs remain correct; historical 41/42/52 boundaries cannot serve as current evidence. Current publication, scan, execution and deployed restore are not proved. | Product owner | Complete source review, then independently satisfy the audit and guarded image publication/scan, migration execution and deployed restore gates. No bypass or deployment approval is implied. |
| D3-R7 | Closed | Development's document runtime is a mixed release (parser `43f69e9`, processor `9240c6b`), which no retained Gateway baseline can describe, so post-apply zero-drift verification and every further Gateway release are blocked. The stale document-dark operator chain also lags the platform (its variable inventory lacks `candidate_deletion_restore_role_enabled`, `d2_community_runtime`, and `outbox_starts_paused`), so it cannot be used to converge the release. | Product owner; implementer | Tracked as D3-020. The owner approved the two-receipt (schema-8) baseline with the convergence-only rule on 2026-09-19; the release ran and converged on 2026-09-20 (see D3-020). Keep parser and processor refreshes together from one receipt from now on. |
| D3-R8 | Open | Account deletion removes only the recorded exact object keys. A normalized object left by an earlier parser version (for example after a parser change between retries of one document) would survive it, and the exact-key inventory cannot see it. | Vrij Patel | Found 2026-09-21 while designing D3-027. Make account deletion verify by the document prefixes too, as its own small task, or fold it into D3-027 slice 3 if the owner prefers. Synthetic data only today. 2026-09-28: two source objects from earlier synthetic runs (`quarantine/019ff70b...` from 2026-08-12 and `quarantine/01a05fc5...` from 2026-09-01, no normalized object) remain in the development documents bucket; whether they belong to live rows or are leftovers of an earlier account deletion is not determinable from outside the database, so they are left untouched and listed here. 2026-09-30: D3-031 implements prefix-wide account cleanup locally with focused object-store and composed PostgreSQL tests. Release and deployed acceptance remain; the two historical unexplained objects are not touched. |
| D3-R9 | Open | The workload permissions boundary denied `s3:DeleteObjectVersion` on the candidate-documents bucket, so account deletion could not purge a candidate's stored resumes either (the candidate-rights acceptance used synthetic candidates without documents). Found 2026-09-21 when the resume purge failed. | Product owner (approves the boundary plan); implementer | Fixed in development 2026-09-21: the owner-reviewed bootstrap plan (change set `candidate-documents-version-delete-v1`) is applied and the resume purge now works. **Still open until** D3-028 adds a documents case to the account-deletion acceptance and it passes (moved from D3-027 slice 5 on 2026-09-28 with the owner's agreement); staging's boundary is unchanged until asked. |
| D3-R10 | Open | A 2026-10-02 friction review found D3-002 and D3-003 each carried an "open" remaining item (resume-AST persistence, tailored-resume version storage) that sat open for over a week and turned out to need only an owner decision ("don't persist it," 2026-09-28), not any code. A task register can overstate remaining engineering work by leaving a decision-shaped item phrased as a build item. | Unassigned | When reviewing an open task, check whether its "remaining" item is actually a design question that just needs an owner answer before assuming it needs new code. No specific other instance is confirmed yet. |
| D3-R11 | Open | Found 2026-10-02 during a friction review: at least two independent, hand-rolled Terraform state-history checks (`operate-development-document-dark-deployment.ps1`'s recovery verification, and the D3-034 gateway-release-guards exact-history check) each assumed a Terraform apply advances the state serial by exactly one, and each broke when a real apply legitimately wrote more than one snapshot (2026-09-30 PRs #470-473, then again 2026-10-01 for D3-034). Each occurrence needed its own bespoke, hand-pinned exception (hashes, serials, timestamps) rather than a shared, general "N consistent snapshots ending in zero drift" check, and the first fix (#472) was even wired into the wrong operator script before #473 corrected it. This is the largest recurring tax found in the friction review: two multi-day debugging loops for the same underlying gap one day apart. | Unassigned | Scope (not yet designed or approved): a single, well-tested shared helper that accepts any number of state snapshots since baseline as long as they form one unbroken lineage with no unexplained actor/content change, and the final snapshot matches the reviewed plan with zero drift - replacing each operator script's own hand-rolled "exactly one serial advance" assumption. This touches deployment-safety code directly, needs its own design review and full regression pass against every existing operator script's guard-test suite before any script is migrated to it, and should not be done as a side effect of an unrelated change. |
| D3-R12 | Open | Isolated cover-letter generation remains unsafe to activate without provider creation restrictions and bounded dispatch/privacy quiescence. A private RPC abort does not stop remote work. Source-only conditional transitions and purge fences have offline and connected PostgreSQL evidence; cleanup now requires explicit private writer/provider quiescence even for pending rows with empty prefixes, but its production confirmation authority is not implemented, but the maintenance role lacks `s3:DeleteObject` for creating generation delete markers, and no deployed fence/runtime proof exists. | Vrij Patel; engineering; security/privacy reviewers | Complete the provider dispatch/quiescence and transient-retention protocol, then obtain separate review of narrowly scoped marker-creation/provider IAM and guarded deployment plans. Preserve default-off until source, deployed and accountable evidence all pass; see proposed ADR-037. |
